single_trivia_dev_3547
The French horse race called the Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe is run at which race course?
A: ["1976 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "1974 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "2017 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "2016 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "1972 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe"]
A: The Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe is a Group 1 flat horse race in France open to thoroughbreds aged three years or older.
A: Exit? No.
A: ["1976 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "1974 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "2017 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "2016 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "1972 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe", "Prix de l'Ope\u0301ra", "Prix Niel", "2013 Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe"]
A: The Prix de l'Arc de Triomphe is a Group 1 flat horse race in France open to thoroughbreds aged three years or older. It is run at Longchamp over a distance of 2,400 metres (about 112 miles).
A: Exit? No.

"The modern British period of history called ""The Winter of Discontent"" began when?"
A: ["Winter of Discontent", "Winter of Discontent", "Winter of Discontent", "Winter of Discontent", "The Winter of Our Discontent"]
A: The Winter of Discontent was the winter of 1978–79 in the United Kingdom, during which there were widespread strikes by public sector trade unions demanding larger pay rises, following the ongoing pay caps of the Labour Party government led by James Callaghan against Trades Union Congress opposition to control inflation, during the coldest winter for 16 years.

So the answer is: 1978.
